## Document 061: 2026年9月通胀数据点评：生产与消费价格修复分化，内需回暖尚有“时间差”

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月通胀数据点评：生产与消费价格修复分化，内需回暖尚有“时间差”.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 10 月 14 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
生产与消费 价格修复分化 ，内需 回暖 尚有 “时间差 ” 
——2026 年 9 月通胀数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：CPI方面，9 月 CPI 同比上涨 0.9%，涨幅较上月扩大0.1个百分点，连
续两个月回升，但低于市场预期。食品价格同比降幅扩大至1.7%，主要受鲜
菜、 鲜果价格下跌影响， 猪价同比跌幅收窄； 非食品价格同比涨幅扩大至1.5%，
主要受国际油价反弹及电子产品价格上涨带动，其中汽油价格同比上涨
16.6%，拉动整体 CPI同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点。环比看，CPI 上涨 0.2%，
涨幅较上月收窄 0.2 个百分点，核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平于 1.0%，服务价格环
比转跌，反映国内终端需求修复仍偏温和。展望10 月，国际油价高位运行及
AI 算力需求带动的消费电子涨价仍将提供支撑， 但蔬果价格同比降幅扩大、 猪
肉价格环比转跌，叠加服务价格季节性回落，CPI 整体或延续温和修复。PPI
方面，9 月 PPI 同比上涨 5.0%， 涨幅较上月扩大1.2个百分点， 高于市场预期，
主要受国际油价反弹及低基数效应推动。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业、 石油
和天然气开采业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大至
35.3%、24.5%和 21.6%，化工、化纤等行业价格亦明显回升，反映能源价格
上涨正沿产业链向中游原材料环节传导；电气机械、 计算机通信及其他电子设
备制造业价格环比均上涨0.4%，AI 算力相关需求形成一定支撑。 环比看，PPI
上涨 1.2%，涨幅较上月扩大 0.8 个百分点，石油相关行业价格上涨是主要拉
动。展望后续，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势、国际油价高位运行、冬季
煤炭备货及低基数效应仍将支撑PPI， 但上下游价格传导不畅、 终端消费偏弱，
生活资料价格上涨空间仍然有限，PPI 修复或延续结构性分化。 
 输入性因素支撑 CPI 同比修复，核心 CPI 保持稳定。同比来看，9 月 CPI 同
比涨幅扩大 0.1个百分点至 0.9%，连续两个月回升，增速低于wind一致预期
（1.02%）。其中，食品价格同比下降1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3个百分点。非食品
价格同比涨幅扩大 0.3 个百分点至 1.5%，主要受能源及电子产品价格涨幅扩
大影响。9 月中东地缘冲突持续发酵， 能源供应风险上升， 国际油价二次反弹，
带动国内能源价格涨幅扩大，对整体CPI 形成支撑。环比来看，9 月 CPI环比
涨幅收窄 0.2个百分点至 0.2%， 其中食品价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现，
非食品价格环比涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.2%。9 月核心 CPI 同比涨幅持平
于 1%， 核心CPI 环比回落至 0%。核心 CPI 连续数月运行在 1.0%附近， 说明
剔除输入性扰动后，国内终端需求的修复力度仍然温和。展望 10 月，国际油
价高位运行及 AI算力需求带动的消费电子价格上涨仍将为CPI提供支撑，但
食品价格仍面临一定下行压力。 同时， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶
段，核心 CPI 修复力度预计仍偏温和。整体来看，输入性因素仍将对CPI 形
成支撑，但内需修复偏缓或限制涨幅，预计10 月 CPI 同比延续温和回升。 
 食品价格同比降幅扩大，蔬果价格下跌是主要拖累。同比来看，9 月食品价格
同比下降 1.7%，降幅扩大 0.3 个百分点，主要受鲜菜、鲜果价格下跌及猪肉
价格仍处低位影响。 食品细项中，鲜菜、鲜果价格同比分别下降8.1%、1.5%，
降幅均扩大，反映季节性供给增加对农产品价格形成压制。牛肉、 羊肉价格同
比分别上升 7.5%、6.4%，涨幅均扩大。猪价同比下降 10.0%，降幅收窄 1.8
个百分点，价格弱势有所缓解，但同比仍处于较大跌幅区间。环比来看，食品
价格环比上升 0.4%，持平上月表现。食品细项中，鲜菜价格环比涨幅收窄5
个百分点至 0.5%，鲜果价格环比由下降转为上升 0.6%。猪价环比涨幅收窄
0.1 个百分点至 1.2%，9 月猪肉均价同比下降 13.58%，降幅继续收窄。从最
新的高频数据走势看，截至 2026年 10 月 13 日，同比方面，10 月初 28 种重
点监测蔬菜价格同比下降 17.1%，7 种重点监测水果价格同比下降 6.1%，同

2
比降幅均扩大。鸡蛋价格同比涨幅收窄至31.7%，猪肉价格同比下降 11.5%，
降幅收窄；环比方面，28 种重点监测蔬菜价格环比由上升转为下降5.9%，7
种重点监测水果价格环比下降 0.3%，降幅收窄。鸡蛋价格环比由上升转为下
降 9.6%，猪肉价格环比由上升转为下降2.2%。结合 10 月初高频数据，蔬果
价格同比降幅进一步扩大， 猪肉价格环比转跌， 食品价格短期仍面临一定下行
压力，猪肉价格修复尚不稳定，后续仍需观察生猪出栏节奏、养殖端产能调整
及消费需求变化。
 能源和电子产品支撑非食品价格， 暑期结束拖累服务价格环比表现。同比来看，
9 月非食品价格同比涨幅扩大0.3个百分点至 1.5%，主要受国际油价回升带动
能源价格上涨影响。9 月汽油价格同比涨幅扩大 7.3 个百分点至 16.6%，仅此
一项即影响 CPI同比上涨约 0.47 个百分点， 带动整体能源价格涨幅从4.1%跃
升至 7.3%。扣除能源的工业消费品价格上涨1.6%，涨幅回落 0.2 个百分点。

其中黄金饰品价格上涨 22.5%，涨幅回落 11.1 个百分点；数据存储设备、平
板电脑、 计算机和移动电话机价格分别上涨46.7%、23.6%、21.8%和 14.9%，
涨幅均有扩大，反映电子产品价格仍有较强支撑。服务价格同比上升 0.8%，
涨幅持平上月。环比来看，9 月非食品价格环比涨幅收窄0.1个百分点至 0.2%。
工业消费品价格上涨 0.7%，涨幅比上月回落 0.1 个百分点，其中国内汽油价
格上涨 4.9%，涨幅比上月回落2.3 个百分点， 黄金饰品价格由上月上涨7.6%
转为下降 2.1%。服务 CPI 环比由上升转为下降 0.2%。服务中， 随着暑期结束，
飞机票、 宾馆住宿和旅行社收费价格分别下降8.7%、7.0%和 6.0%； 受成本走
高影响，移动电话机、计算机、数据存储设备和平板电脑价格分别上涨 3.7%、
2.0%、1.7%和 1.5%。展望 10 月，非食品价格预计延续分化走势，同比仍受
到能源价格回升和电子产品涨价支撑， 但环比上涨动能可能进一步减弱。 一方

面， 国际油价同比改善以及电子产品价格上涨仍将支撑非食品价格表现； 另一
方面， 暑期结束后服务价格进入季节性回落阶段， 叠加黄金等前期涨价品类涨
幅收窄，非食品价格环比或继续放缓。
 油价反弹带动 PPI超预期回升，资源品价格韧性较强。从同比看，受国际油价
涨幅回升及低基数效应影响，PPI同比涨幅扩大 1.2 个百分点至 5.0%，增速高
于 wind 一致预期 （4.3%）。生产资料同比涨幅扩大1.5 个百分点至 6.5%，生
活资料同比下降 0.1%，降幅收窄 0.4 个百分点。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选
业同比涨幅扩大 8.7个百分点至 35.3%，主要受冬季供暖备煤需求增加、 主产
矿区供应阶段性偏紧、国际煤价上涨预期效应等因素影响。石油和天然气开采
业、石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格同比涨幅分别扩大14.0、10.5 个百分点
至 24.5%和 21.6%。化学纤维制造业、 化学原料及化学制品制造业同比涨幅分
别扩大至 12.3%和 12.1%。一方面， 国际原油价格上涨直接推升采掘和炼化环

节价格；另一方面，石油作为基础原材料，通过燃料、化工品等渠道向中下游
传导， 带动化学原料、 化纤等行业价格上涨。从环比看，PPI环比涨幅扩大 0.8
个百分点至 1.2%， 生产资料环比涨幅扩大 1 个百分点至 1.4%， 生活资料环比
涨幅收窄 0.1 个百分点至 0.1%。分行业看，煤炭开采和洗选业环比涨幅扩大
6.7 个百分点至 9.5%；石油相关行业价格上涨动能显著增强。 石油和天然气开
采业、 石油煤炭及其他燃料加工业价格环比涨幅分别扩大4.3、4.9个百分点至
10.4%和 8.4%。电气机械和器材制造业、计算机通信和其他电子设备制造业
价格环比均上涨 0.4%，AI 与算电协同带来的结构性需求推动资本开支扩张。
国际层面，10 月 13 日布伦特原油现货价格为130.92美元/桶，10 月初油价同
比涨幅扩大 28.36 个百分点至 100.08%。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡
的紧张局势持续升温，伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制，美国持续强化对伊朗石
油贸易的封锁，区域供应风险上升。原油市场供给担忧升温，国际油价或维持

高位，并通过能源、化工等产业链向国内工业品价格传导。9 月，PPI 同比涨
幅（5.0%）与 CPI 同比涨幅（0.9%）之间的差距达到 4.1 个百分点，工厂端
的原材料和能源成本正在快速上涨，但尚未同步传导至终端消费市场，中下游
企业的利润空间正受到挤压。后续来看，国际油价高位震荡、冬季煤炭备货叠
加低基数构成后续 PPI 的核心推升力量。石油与煤炭开采价格同比或继续走
阔，并向化工、化纤等中下游传导；AI 算力相关电气设备、通信设备制造价
格或维持增长。 但上下游传导仍不畅， 终端消费偏弱或仍制约生活资料涨价空
间。
 风险提示：国内政策落地节奏不及预期，国际大宗商品价格波动超预期， 海外
地缘扰动风险。

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track

### Page 2

CCL for PCB: spec upgrades well on track
toward CY27/28 with pricing upside
Migration in raw materials of CCL
Spec advancements in resin more debatable
Major raw materials for building CCL (copper clad laminate) include cloth, resin, and
copper foil. Glass cloth serves as grounding and offers support to the CCL and PCB
(printed circuit board) structure, and it is immersed in resins built on different in-house
chemical recipes by CCL makers to obtain PP (prepreg). Afterwards, CCL makers

add/laminate the copper foil on this intermediate product (PP), to form the CCL supplied
to PCB customers. Across the previous generations (Exhibit 2), we have seen rising
penetration of PPE/PPO (polyphenylene ether/oxide) gradually replacing FR-4/ epoxy
resins throughout M4-M8 grade CCL, while hydrocarbon resin gradually gains traction
from M8-grade CCL. Yet, there is a debate on PTFE-based (polytetrafluoroethylene) CCL
vs hydrocarbon resin as the CCL grade advances into M9/M10.

As illustrated in Exhibit 4, we see a more diversified path of resin upgrades from M8-
grade CCL, while the advancement in copper foil and glass cloth seems to be more
certain. In general, we note that the mix of PPO vs hydrocarbon used is roughly 2:1 in
M8-grade CCL, and such proportion reverses to 1:2 in M9-grade CCL. Yet, the broader
range of resin options could potentially alter the competitive dynamics within the CCL
industry, since different vendors are promoting (or have edge) in different types of CCL

solutions. Or in other words, the likely penetration by PTFE-based solutions (pushed
mainly by NVIDIA) will favor the suppliers with experiences in handling relevant products
(though smaller market size such as base station), while, at the same time, it might
curtail the headroom for major Taiwanese suppliers, including EMC and TUC (Taiwan
Union), to grow.
PPO/BMI vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9; hydrocarbon vs PTFE in M9/M10
In our view, hydrocarbon resin exhibits an ultra-low dielectric constant (Dk) of 2.2-2.6

and a dissipation factor (Df) reaching 0.001-0.005, as low as 0.0005 at high frequencies
(e.g., 10 GHz and above), thanks to its non-polar molecular backbone. However, there are
drawbacks to hydrocarbon resin, including rather limited flexibility and high brittleness
after curing, which necessitate the mix with ductile polymers such as PPO or BMI
(bismaleimide) to improve the toughness (peel strength). Nonetheless, the performance
in Dk/Df would be reduced after adding PPO/BMI to hydrocarbon resin, which makes the

solution not that favorable in meeting customers’ demand/requirement.
Within hydrocarbon resin, we note there are two major advanced versions, including ODV
(oxidative vulcanization; lower-end) and BCB (benzocyclobutene; higher-end), and key
suppliers for the latter include Dow Chemical and Asahi Kasei. In terms of Df, <0.0008
can be reached by BCB type of hydrocarbon resin based on the standard of M9-grade
CCL, and the number would hit <0.0005 based on the standard of M9+/M10-grade. By

CCL suppliers, we note EMC (Elite Material) adopts hydrocarbon/BMI resin in M9-grade
CCL, while Panasonic blends PPO or BMI (with hydrocarbon) in M9 (vs PPO/hydrocarbon
in M7/M8). Shengyi Technology also adopts PPO resin in M9-grade offering.
Given the disadvantages (discussed above) of PPO resin, some CCL providers
alternatively also adopt mPPO (modified PPO; Exhibit 5) by blending the PPO with other
resins (e.g., epoxy methacrylate, styrene). By doing so, the overall dielectric and thermal

properties of the composite are enhanced, and the reduced molecular weight improves
solubility in solvents like toluene and MEK (methyl ethyl ketone). This in turn enables the
resin to thermoset, and it becomes more suitable for CCL processing. By application, we
believe it is adopted in the LPU (language processing unit) and UBB (universal
baseboard) under Vera Rubin platform, 1.6T networking switch, and will also be
potentially (competing with PTFE-based solutions) adopted in the backplane.

### Page 3

Similar to what we have seen in PPO/PPE vs hydrocarbon in M8/M9, we believe
hydrocarbon and PTFE will coexist and complement each other in M9/M10-grade CCL,
while the sheer adoption on one side is unlikely, given 1) different consideration on
cost/performance by different customers; and 2) the difficulty in sourcing across resin
and the adjacent equipment in producing different types of CCL (e.g. vacuum hot press
machine). Meanwhile, we note that PPO/PPE cannot be paired with PTFE given the

conflicts in characteristics.
In addition to the specialized plasma treatment, and long lead time in pressing machine
(operating under high temperature) with high costs, one of the other major drawbacks to
PTFE is the lack of stiffness in texture which makes it hard to be processed under PCB
drilling/lamination. Thus, SiO2 (silicon dioxide) is used as a filler (or with increasing
consumption vs that in M7/M8-grade CCL) to fill the resin matrix, and this helps reduce

the thermal expansion coefficient (CTE) and enhance the mechanical strength of the
composites. Elsewhere, a lack of adhesion to copper foil is the other key technological
challenge when processing PTFE-based solutions, and this requires the help from
hydrocarbon resin (to enhance the adhesion).
In short, the pursuit of more advanced materials like PTFE/hydrocarbon will
simultaneously incur other technological challenges, which, in our view, makes relevant

solutions to become more versatile (to solve the associated challenges). In Exhibit 6, we
present the promoted solutions of M9/M10-grade CCL in the market, and we compare
different metrics, including signal loss, mechanical strength, processibility, and costs.
Given the low penetration by M9-grade CCL currently (evidenced by single-digit% of
industry leader EMC’s revenue), we believe none of these solutions has become
mainstream. In other words, we deem the ongoing development and qualification/testing

results worth monitoring, and these include the feedback from board, ODM (original
design manufacturer) and end-customers’ levels. In our view, the most imminent (and
perhaps critical) one will likely be the solution adopted by Rubin Ultra switch board and
backplane (if any), which could be finally confirmed by end-2026/early-2027.

Exhibit 5: A comparison between PPO and mPPO 
mPPO carries enhanced processability and reactivity when compared with PPO 
Characteristics/type of 
resin PPO mPPO 
Dk Inherently low. Values for PPO-based composites are typically in the range of 2.1 - 3.5.  Retains low Dk, with high-performance grades achieving tightly controlled values, e.g., 2.8 - 3.0 for M9 grade. 
Df Inherently very low. Values for PPO-based systems can be as low as 0.0006. Significantly reduced and optimized. Ranges from 0.004 – 0.008 for M6/M7 grades down to <0.002 (typically 0.0015–0.002) for ultra-low loss M9 grades. 
Processability Limited. High melt viscosity and lack of reactivity make it difficult to process.  Better. Low molecular weight and reactive end-groups enable good solubility, compatibility with CCL manufacturing, and thermosetting cross-linking. 
Reactivity Low. Lacks sufficient reactive groups to cross-link effectively with other resins. High. Functionalized end-groups (e.g., styrene, methacrylate) allow it to chemically bond with other components in the resin formulation. 
Source: BofA Global Research

Clearer upgrades in glass cloth and copper foil  
While the upgrades in resin system appear to be more debatable with a versatility in 
solutions, we witness a firmer roadmap advancement in glass cloth and copper foil. For 
glass cloth, (see reports) Nittobo shared the view of 100% demand growth in NER this 
year (FY3/27), followed by another c60% growth next year. As for copper foil, Mitsui 
Kinzoku also revised up the mix of HVLP4 and above products in 1H26 (within very 
smooth profile copper foil) to 45% (was 40%), and the figure is expected to reach 60% 
in 2H26. On pricing, we note that Mitsui Kinzoku also mentioned it would try pursuing a 
higher ASP in product segments where demand remains strong, while there is a higher 
processing fee (Exhibit 7) charged by copper foil suppliers in the high-end copper foil 
market. All-in-all, we see positive indications to the high-end CCL pricing, given that the 
CCL makers will try to pass through the inflated costs (due to stronger demand and the 
premium in processing fee) to the product ASP.

Ongoing spec migration on multiple fronts into CY27-28 
We see a clear upgrade cycle for CCL materials and PCB production technique across 
networking switches and AI (artificial intelligence) accelerators, including GPUs (graphic 
processing unit) and ASICs (application specific integrated circuits). In our view, the spec 
advancement (Exhibit 8) is primarily driven by faster data rates and increasingly 
stringent signal-integrity requirements. For NVIDIA, GB200/GB300 largely adopt M8-
grade CCL, with low-Dk1/2 glass cloth and HVLP2/3 copper foil. The transition to VR200 
drives further CCL specification upgrades toward M8+/M9-grade, along with the 
adoption of HVLP4 copper foil in VR200 compute board and midplane, while LPU adopts 
Q cloth with less signal loss (vs low Dk2).  
While NVIDIA tends to place greater emphasis on glass cloth upgrades, ASICs and 
networking switches are more focused on copper foil migration. Among ASICs, we 
expect the CCL spec for Google TPU (tensor processing unit) v8 to migrate to M8+

based on low Dk2 cloth and HVLP3 copper foil (vs M7-grade CCL with low Dk1 and
HVLP2 for TPU v7), followed by M9-grade adoption in TPU v9 based on HVLP4 copper
foil (with either low Dk2 or Q-cloth). As for the Trainium chips by Amazon, there is a
more visible upgrade in copper foil (vs glass cloth) taking place in Trainium 3 (2026),
while its OAM (open accelerator module) in Trainium 4 (2027) will likely be upgraded to
M8-grade CCL based on low Dk2 cloth. Simultaneously, we also expect a migration

toward HDI (high-density interconnect) from HLC (high-layer count), with regard to the
PCB production for the UBB (universal baseboard) in TPU v9 and Trainium 4.

Along with the data-rate migration toward 800G and beyond (from 100/200G) for optical
module, PCB structure evolves from conventional tenting design toward more
complicated mSAP/SAP (semi-additive process) with higher layer count, enabling finer
L/S (line/space) and smaller pitch (Exhibit 9). To elaborate, L/S of 3.2T optical module
should shrink materially to 15/15μm from 60/60μm at 800G, and it was 125/125um for

100G generation. Simultaneously, the layer count would also increase to up to layers for
3.2T products, vs 10-layer in 400G generation.
Amid the backdrop of rapid spec migration, major PCB suppliers are accelerating
capacity expansion to capture the burgeoning demand in high-end optical module
(Exhibit 10), and these include Avary under ZDT (Zhen Ding Technology), Founder, SCC
(Shennan Circuits), Unimicron and Compeq. While Unimicron remains the biggest

supplier of optical module PCB (printed circuit board) in 2H26, we flag Avary and SCC
could potentially become the other major suppliers in 2027, given their more aggressive
capacity increase.

Expect further ASP uplift driven by mix & cost inflation 
We expect CCL (for PCB) names’ revenue growth to be fueled by continuous capacity 
expansion and ASP uplifts, and these include EMC, TUC, Shengyi Tech and Kingboard 
Laminates among our coverage. The ASP increase, in our view, will be driven by both 
price hike on a like-for-like basis (given the cost push) and mix improvements to higher 
CCL grades, and we note the former/latter represent bigger boost to the near-
term/long-term ASP. To elaborate, the difference in near-term vs long-term boost can 
be explained by 1) a shorter production cycle time of less than 1-2 weeks by CCL makers 
which normally carry less than a quarter of inventory; and 2) a continuous spec 
advancement in CCL material grade given the requirement in data-transmission.  
In Exhibit 11-13, we decipher the revenue growth by different CCL names based on the 
planned capacity expansion and ASP uplift, as well as the assumption of 95%/90%/90% 
capacity utilization rate by EMC/TUC/Shengyi Tech in 2028. In our view, the implied ASP 
growth of 4%/11%/18% by respective names look conservative, and we see room for 
further upside in the case of continuous cost inflation from the raw materials (e.g. glass 
cloth, copper foil). Further, we believe a smaller magnitude of ASP increase by EMC (vs 
TUC/Shengyi Tech) in 2028 can be explained by its already significant exposure to high-
end CCL market in 2027. For reference, we present the CCL ASP by each grade with the 
figure for M4-grade indexed to 100 in Exhibit 20, and the gap of ASP by each grade 
remains quite meaningful, in our view.  
Regarding the CCL mix by grade, we break down the revenue contribution by different 
grades to each company throughout 2025-28, and we compare the numbers to their 
gross margins in Exhibit 14-19. When comparing the gross margin, we carve out the 
revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL, given that M6-grade is already in supply-
demand balance (or slightly oversupply) situation from late-2025.

### Page 8

Among the three names, we expect EMC to remain the industry leader with 
73%/85%/92% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 2026/27/28, which helps 
strengthen its gross margin to 35.8%/36.7% in 2027/28 (vs 34% in 2026). TUC will 
follow suit with 43%/47%/53% revenue mix from M7-grade and above CCL in 
2026/27/28, with gross margin edging toward 32.6%/33.7% in 2027/28 (vs 30.4% in 
2026). As for Shengyi Tech, we expect 28%/35%/57% revenue mix from M7-grade and 
above CCL in 2026/27/28, and its gross margin (in CCL business) is expected to inch up 
to 31%/31.8 in 2027/28 (vs 30.7% in 2026).

There is ~50%/100% price premium by M8.5/M9 vs M8

Source: BofA Global Research  
 BofA GLOBAL RESEARCH 
Regarding the ASP uplifts by firm in 2027, we further decode into the factors of cost 
inflation and mix enhancement as shown in Exhibit 21. To simply put, we expect a 
smaller boost to EMC (vs TUC/Shengyi Tech) from cost inflation of raw materials and 
the corresponding pass-through, given its relatively lower revenue exposure to low-/mid-
end CCL market, where CCL makers pass through the cost more frequently upon 
squeezed margin profile. Yet, on the other hand, EMC (in terms of ASP) is expected to 
benefit more from the enhanced mix in 2027, thanks to the further ramp of M8/M8.5-
grade CCL as well as the penetration increase of M9 and PTFE-based solutions.  
Year-to-date, we believe there is already 40%+/20%+ price hike across low-/high-end 
CCL, and we do not rule out the likelihood of further price increase given the (see report) 
expectations of continuously rising copper price into late-2026 and 2027 (Exhibit 22). 
There was a price hike of VSP (very smooth profile) foil, effective from July 2026 by 
Mitsui Kinzoku given the demand strength and likely to be followed suit by a Taiwanese 
supplier (Co-Tech), based on our checks with supply chain. On the glass cloth side, we 
note the potential pricing upside from low Dk2 cloth given the supply shortage, while the 
price of low-end E-glass cloth (such as 7628 type) remains on the uptrend (Exhibit 23) 
and may reach CNY15-16 (per msq) by end-2026. In our view, all of these point to a 
benign pricing outlook for CCL makers.  
To pinpoint the benefits from price increase by CCL makers, we conduct a scenario 
analysis to each name’s 2027 bottom-line as shown in Exhibit 24. In short, we expect 
1.2-1.6% upside to the net income estimates coming from every 1ppt price hike, if such 
price increase purely reflects cost inflation. On the other hand, we expect 3.3-5.1% 
upside to the net income estimates, if the price hike is purely driven a supply shortage 
(which enables CCL makers to raise the ASP proactively). Further on Shengyi Tech, we 
assume that its PCB business (Shengyi Electronics) can also pass on the cost increase 
(from CCL side), with same magnitude to its ASP.  
For Kingboard Laminates, its vertical integration significantly enhances its earnings 
sensitivity to price hikes, as most of its key raw materials are produced in-house. 
Notably, price hikes in glass fiber cloth can translate almost entirely into profit, given in-
house production of yarn. Our analysis suggests that 100% increase in CCL prices could 
drive c.28ppt gross margin expansion for KBL, and more than 200% growth at the EPS 
level in 2026. This assumes that future price hikes are primarily driven by cloth 
tightness, while input costs for copper foil and resin remain relatively stable.

Sector view: stay positive on industry leaders  
We stay constructive on the CCL for PCB area, given the ongoing spec upgrades and 
robust demand growth into 2027-28, and we believe the worries from CPO (co-packaged 
optics) proliferation are either overstated or distant, as the mass adoption of CPO to 
replace NPO (near-packaged optics) or pluggable optical modules will not take place until 
2029/30, at earliest.  
Currently, we see extended lead-time (of production) toward 2-3 months by high-end 
CCL makers in 2H26 (vs 1-2 months seen in 1H26), while low/mid-end suppliers’ lead 
time is also extended to 1-2 months (7-10 days before). We also believe the current 
valuation by EMC (21-22x 2027E P/E) and TUC (17-18x 2027E P/E) looks compelling, 
given that the numbers do not seem lofty when compared with the historical trading 
range of 1) 15-20x before mid-2023; and 2) normally above 20x after mid-2023 when 
the strong demand from AI server starts to emerge. As for Kingboard Laminates, it is 
traded at 8x 2027E P/E, below historical average of 10x. 
Among our coverage, we highlight Buy-rated EMC, given its technology leadership in 
high-end CCL area along with diversified customer profile across GPU and ASIC 
accounts, as well as TUC, given its (see report) aggressive capacity expansion into 2027 
and beyond. We also highlight Buy rating on Doosan Corp and Kingboard Laminates, 
given the former’s solid position in NVIDIA GPU and Amazon switch, as well as the 
latter’s robust earnings expansion amid CCL price hike and rising high-end contribution 
starting 2027. As for Shengyi Tech, we remain relatively conservative, given its relatively 
slower expansion in CCL capacity along with a milder growth in the PCB business.

EMC (2383 TT; Buy; PO NT$8,200) 
We reckon EMC’s potential share loss in Rubin Ultra switch board (tray), given the 
competition in PTFE-based (hybrid) solutions (vs M9-Q), as well as the ongoing debate 
between PTFE and quartz-glass/hydrocarbon resin solutions into M10-grade CCL. We 
also note that M9-Q (where EMC has more notable technology leadership) is likely to be 
adopted in backplane by NVIDIA, no matter if it will initially be seen in Rubin Ultra or 
Feynman generation. Net-net, we believe the impact on bottom-line (if any) from 
aforementioned headwinds should be confined to LSD (low-single-digit)%, considering 
1) 80-90k (per month) of relevant demand by NVIDIA’s switch board vs EMC’s capacity 
of ~6/9mn in 2026/27; and 2) the demand backfill from other high-end projects across 
AMD and other ASIC customers.  
Looking ahead, we see further expansion plan beyond 1H28 as a catalyst, if it is 
announced. In addition to the CCL for PCB business, we note that substrate CCL 
business boasts at least 50-60% GM (gross margin), and EMC could expand relevant 
capacity to 1.2mn sheet /month which could help expedite its market-share gain toward 
~5% (vs 1-2% in 2025). We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 5%/11%/14% after penciling in 
stronger revenue growth and gross margin profile, while our PO stays at NT$8,200 as 
we apply a lower P/E multiple of 30x (was 34x), with the same valuation period of 2H27-
1H28E, mainly reflecting the overhang from competition in PTFE-based solutions.

TUC (6274 TT; Buy; PO NT$2,600) 
Looking into 2027, we flag multiple positives from spec advancements for TUC, 
including 1) the potentially partial adoption of M9-grade CCL by 1.6T networking switch, 
where TUC’s solution is based on low Dk3 glass cloth; 2) the upgrade to M8-grade CCL 
for LEO (low earth orbit); and 3) the migration toward M8-grade material by Vera CPU 
(central processing unit). Meanwhile, we share the view that hybrid solution from PTFE-
based CCL may last toward M10-grade. Demand from high-end optical module (800G 
and above) could have remained underestimated at current stage, as we believe relevant 
TAM (total addressable market) for CCL could reach NT$30bn+, which represents close 
to 50% of TUC’s total revenue in 2026. In terms of Thailand operations, we believe it 
could turn breakeven or profitable from 3Q26, thanks to the smooth ramp of 
capacity/output to pair with the robust demand growth outlook from customers.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 7%/11%/7% after baking in the assumptions of stronger 
revenue growth and slightly enhanced GM profile as well as operating leverage. Our PO 
stays at NT$2,600, based on a lower P/E multiple of 27x (was 30.5x) and an unchanged 
valuation period of 2H27-1H28E. We attribute the cut in valuation multiple to the likely 
growing traction by PTFE-based solutions that serve as other alternatives in the high-
end CCL market.

Shengyi Technology (600183 CH; U/P; PO CNY99) 
We believe revenue contribution from PTFE-based CCL could reach ~10% based on the 
assumption of US$300-400 ASP per piece, and we expect the ramp of such business to 
bolster the overall margin profile thanks to ~50% GM by relevant solutions. Yet we also 
note that the firm’s capacity for M6-grade and above CCL remains relatively limited at 
~10% mix currently, and further capacity ramp will be dependent on the progress in 
Thailand and Songshan Lake (for M7-M9), after its Jiangxi phase 2 capacity became fully 
loaded after 1H26.  
We raise 2026/27/28E EPS by 29%/21%/18% after baking in 2Q26 results and the 
assumptions of stronger revenue growth and margin profiles, which also reflect its 
further share gain in NVIDIA’s AI server system/platforms. Our PO goes to CNY99 (from 
CNY83), same based on 24x 2027E P/E, and the volatility risk rating is changed to C 
from B.

Valuation discussion  
Both Buy-rated EMC and TUC are expected to post stronger EPS growth toward 2028 
along with a higher dividend yield. However, they trade at lower P/E multiples when 
compared with peers’ average in CCL industry. As for P/B multiples, the premium 
valuation by these two names, in our view, can be explained by much higher ROE 
expected in 2027-28. On the other hand, our more conservative view on Shengyi can be 
supported by its more stretched valuation (for both P/E and P/B) vs milder EPS growth 
and lower dividend yield, when compared with the peers’ average. Overall, we believe the 
(see report) overhang from CPO proliferation is already largely reflected in the valuation 
of CCL names, which we believe also renders an attractive opportunity for EMC/TUC, 
considering their solid earnings growth ahead fueled by continuous mix upgrades and 
more aggressive capacity expansion.

Price objective basis & risk 
Doosan Corp (DOSAF) 
Our PO of W1,800,000 is derived from SOTP valuation, which implies a fair market cap 
of W32tn = W20tn for core businesses (based on 20x 2027-28E P/E, which is in line with

### Page 22

the average of CCL peers) + W12tn in listed equity investments (Doosan Enerbility, 
Doosan Robotics, and Oricom) + net debt (breakeven) + other investments (W0.25tn, 
mostly non-listed affiliates). Our W1,800,000 PO implies 8x 2027-28E P/B, which 
appears much higher than the 2023-25 average of 4x, as well as a relatively high P/E 
(31x based on 2027-28E EPS) versus other Korea Holdco companies (5-10x). Key reason 
behind our relatively higher fair multiples compared to its historical average and its 
peers should be Doosan's rapidly growing CCL business thanks to its dominant market 
share position within NVIDIA.

Upside risks: (1) further market share gains in high-end CCL for major US hyperscalers, 
(2) a more favorable CCL pricing environment driven by sustained demand growth and 
tight industry supply, (3) incremental demand opportunities from emerging technologies 
such as co-packaged optics (CPO) and other networking equipment, and (4) faster-than-
expected capacity ramp-up.

Downside risks: (1) a slowdown in AI infrastructure spending or cuts to US hyperscaler 
capex, which could weaken demand for high-end CCL used in AI servers and networking 
equipment, (2) intensifying competition and pricing pressure in the advanced CCL 
market, and (3) potential delays in Doosan's CCL capacity expansion. 
Elite Material (ETMCF) 
Our PO of NT$8,200 is based on 30x 2H27-1H28E P/E, at the mid-to-high-end of its 
historical range of 6-34x since 2016. In our view, the multiple is appropriate, given the 
market-share expansion in server/networking, where we see structural growth in 
addressable market.

Upside risks are (1) share gain at substrate CCL, (2) stronger 5G smartphone shipment, 
(3) greater 5G smartphone spec upgrades, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
change.

Downside risks are (1) share loss at iPhone, (2) keener competition in high-end CCL, and 
(3) unfavorable FX and raw material cost change. 
Kingboard Laminates (KGBLF) 
We set our PO at HK$65, on 13x 2H26-1H27E P/E. 13x is around +0.5SD of KBL's 
historical trading range, and matches with its last earnings upcycle in 2021 (led by e-
glass price hike) while also reflects rising mix from high-end AI-related deliveries.

Downside risks: (1) slower-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) better-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) slower-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) slower-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales.

Upside risks: (1) stronger-than-expected CCL price hike, (2) slower-than-expected 
industry supply of upstream materials esp. cloth, (3) stronger-than-expected AI server 
demand, and (4) faster-than-expected external glass fiber cloth sales. 
Shengyi (DGUNF) 
Our PO of CNY99 is based on 24x 2027E P/E, close to the mid-to-high-end range of the 
historical average of 10-35x in 2017-2024. In our view, such valuation multiple is backed 
by operating margin and ROE of 23% and 37% in 2026-28E (vs 11% and 12% in 2023-
25).

Upside risks to our price objective are: (1) better macro conditions, (2) faster 5G 
deployment, (3) less-intensive competition, and (4) favorable FX and raw material cost 
changes.

### Page 23

Downside risks are: (1) worse macro conditions, (2) slower 5G deployment, (3) more 
severe competition, and (4) unfavorable FX and raw material cost changes. 
Taiwan Union Technology Corporation (TWUNF) 
Our PO NT$2,600 is based on 27x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at the mid-to-high-end of 
the historical trading range of 8-31x during 2016-2026. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is justified, given operating margin and ROE expansion toward 26% and 63% in 
2026-28E (vs 13% and 16% in 2023-25) along with robust free-cash flow generation in 
over the same period.

Downside risks are 
1) Fluctuation in raw material prices, which could lead to a more volatile gross margin 
for TUC in the near term 
2) More intensified competition in CCL, which could pressure TUC's overall margin 
profile 
3) Adoption of new type of upstream material (e.g., quartz), which could bring business 
uncertainty for the names in CCL industry, including TUC 
4) Worse-than-expected execution by the company, which could cause market-share loss 
and/or decreased business traction with customers

FUNDAMENTAL EQUITY OPINION KEY: Opinions include a Volatility Risk Rating, an Investment Rating and an Income Rating. VOLATILITY RISK RATINGS, indicators 
of potential price fluctuation, are: A - Low, B - Medium and C - High. INVESTMENT RATINGS reflect the analyst’s assessment of both a stock’s absolute total return 
potential as well as its attractiveness for investment relative to other stocks within its Coverage Cluster (defined below). Our investment ratings are: 1 -  Buy stocks 
are expected to have a total return of at least 10% and are the most attractive stocks in the coverage cluster; 2 - Neutral stocks are expected to remain flat or 
increase in value and are less attractive than Buy rated stocks and 3 - Underperform stocks are the least attractive stocks in a coverage cluster. An investment rating 
of 6 (No Rating) indicates that a stock is no longer trading on the basis of fundamentals. Analysts assign investment ratings considering, among other things, the 0-
12 month total return expectation for a stock and the firm’s guidelines for ratings dispersions (shown in the table below). The current price objective for a stock 
should be referenced to better understand the total return expectation at any given time. The price objective reflects the analyst ’s view of the potential price 
appreciation (depreciation).

## Document 112: 9月新增信贷社融双双超预期，政府债券发行提速，居民融资需求仍偏弱

Source PDF: all report pdf/9月新增信贷社融双双超预期，政府债券发行提速，居民融资需求仍偏弱.pdf
Report source: Unknown
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

9 ⽉新增⼈⺠币贷款 1.08 万亿、社融 3.55 万亿，双双超预期。政府债券发⾏加快是主要⽀
撑；企业短期融资延续偏弱，财政相关配套融资改善拉动企业中⻓期贷款好转；居⺠短
贷、中⻓贷均延续少增，指向地产、消费等经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。后续货币宽松还是⼤
⽅向，降准降息仍有可能，关注地产销售能否回暖。
摘要：
事件：2026 年 9 ⽉新增⼈⺠币贷款 1.08 万亿，前值 600 亿，预期 9880 亿，去年同期 1.29 万亿；新
增社融 3.55 万亿，前值 1.66 万亿，预期 3.13 万亿，去年同期 3.53 万亿；存量社融增速 7.1% ，前
值 7.2% ； M2 同⽐ 7.2% ，预期 7.5% ，前值 7.5% ； M1 同⽐ 3.2% ，预期 3.9% ，前值 4.1% 。
核⼼观点：9 ⽉新增信贷社融规模双双超预期，政府债券发⾏加快是主要⽀撑；企业短期融资延
续偏弱，财政相关配套融资改善拉动企业中⻓期贷款好转；居⺠短贷、中⻓贷均延续少增，指
向地产、消费等经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。此外，虽然政府债券发⾏加快，但从 M2 增速回落、财

政存款同⽐少减来看，财政资⾦落地仍有待加快，政策性⾦融⼯具投放也未有明显提速。往后
看，继续提⽰：政策短期重⼼应会强化抓落实、 “ ⽤好⽤⾜ ” 存量政策，包括加快政府债券、重⼤
项⽬和贴息政策落地等，后续更多增量政策也应会 “ 及时谋划 ” 。具体到货币端， “ 加⼤逆周期调
节⼒度 ” 定调下，宽松还是⼤⽅向，降准降息仍有可能。短期重点关注 9.28 以来⼀揽⼦稳增⻓政
策落地效果、尤其是地产销售能否回暖。
1 、整体看， 9 ⽉新增信贷社融规模双双超预期，但仍低于季节性，结构上未有明显改善。
2 、往后看，继续提⽰：货币宽松还是⼤⽅向，降准降息仍有可能。据 Q3 货政例会强调 “ 加⼤逆
周期调节⼒度 ” 、删除了 “ 加⼤跨周期调节⼒度 ” ，指向当前经济承压背景下，货币政策更加注重
“ 逆周期调节 ” ， 9.29 央⾏已宣布调降 PSL 利率等⼀揽⼦货币政策，后续降准降息仍有可能，重点
关注基本⾯的演化情况。
3 、短期看，有三点关注： 1 ）美伊局势、油价⾛势； 2 ） 9.28 以来⼀揽⼦稳增⻓政策的落地效

果，尤其是地产销售能否回暖； 3 ）财政政策落地效果，资⾦端关注政府债券发⾏节奏、政策性
⾦融⼯具落地情况，项⽬端关注重⼤项⽬部署、 “ 六⽹建设 ” 、基建实物⼯作量等。
正⽂如下：
1 、新增信贷略好于预期，但仍低于季节性，结构也未有好转，经济内⽣动能仍然偏弱。居⺠短
贷、中⻓期贷款均延续少增，指向消费、地产等经济内⽣动能仍偏弱。企业短期融资延续偏
弱，中⻓期融资边际好转，主要应与财政加快落地带动相关配套融资改善有关。
> 总量看， 9 ⽉新增信贷 1.08 万亿，同⽐少增 2100 亿，略好于预期（预期 9880 亿），但明显低于
季节性（近三年同期均为 1.73 万亿）。其中，居⺠部⻔新增贷款规模为 0 ，同⽐少增 3890 亿；企
业部⻔新增贷款 1.29 万亿，同⽐多增 700 亿；⾮银⾦融机构新增贷款 -1485 亿，同⽐少减 863 亿。
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

> 居⺠加杠杆意愿仍不⾜，短贷、中⻓期贷款均延续少增，指向消费、地产等经济内⽣动能仍偏
弱。居⺠短期贷款为 -900 亿，同⽐少增 2321 亿，指向居⺠消费意愿仍偏弱。居⺠中⻓期贷款由
负转正⾄ 874 亿，同⽐少增 1626 亿，与同期房地产销售表现⼀致（ 9 ⽉ 30 ⼤中城商品房销售⾯积
同⽐ -10.8% 、降幅较上⽉进⼀步⾛阔， 11 城⼆⼿房销售⾯积同⽐由正转负⾄ -3.1% ）。后续关注
地产新政落地后能否带动销售边际回暖，从⾼频来看， 10 ⽉地产销售⽬前暂未看到明显改善迹
象。
> 企业短期融资延续偏弱，中⻓期融资边际好转，主要应与财政加快落地带动相关配套融资改善
有关。短期融资⽅⾯，企业短期贷款增加 4600 亿、同⽐少增 2500 亿；票据融资为 -500 亿，同⽐
少减 3526 亿，主要与去年同期企业信贷表现较好、票据冲量需求下降有关。企业中⻓期贷款增
加 8900 亿、同⽐少增 200 亿，表现略弱于季节性（近三年同期均值为 1.04 万亿），可能与近期财
政发⼒加快后，相关配套融资改善有关，反映实体企业融资意愿的 BCI 投资前瞻指数仍然延续下

⾏。

### Page 3

2 、新增社融规模好于预期，但弱于季节性，政府债券同⽐多增是主要拉动，存量社融增速较上
⽉回落 0.1 个百分点⾄ 7.1% 。
> 总量看， 9 ⽉新增社融 3.55 万亿，同⽐多增 192 亿，好于市场预期（市场预期 3.13 万亿），但仍
弱于季节性（近三年同期均值为 3.81 万亿），存量社融增速较上⽉回落 0.1 个百分点⾄ 7.1% 。
> 结构看， 9 ⽉社融⼝径⼈⺠币贷款增加 1.27 万亿，同⽐少增 3342 亿，是社融的主要拖累项。政
府债券新增 1.53 万亿，同⽐多增 3438 亿，指向政府债券发⾏明显加快。企业债券新增 -425 亿，
同⽐少增 561 亿，与企业短期融资放缓表现⼀致。表外融资⽅⾯，表外三项增加 2477 亿，同⽐少
增 1102 亿，表外票据增加 2153 亿、同⽐少增 1081 亿，主要与去年基数偏⾼有关；委托贷款新增
802 亿，指向政策性⾦融⼯具落地，但进度仍慢。

### Page 4

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
3 、 M1 同⽐回落，基数⾛⾼、实体资⾦活化有限是主要拖累； M2 同⽐回落，财政投放偏慢、实
体信⽤扩张放缓是主要拖累。
>9 ⽉ M1 同⽐ 3.2% 、较上⽉回落 0.9 个百分点，基数⾛⾼是主要拖累，实体资⾦活化也未能形成
有效⽀撑。 M2 同⽐ 7.2% ，较上⽉回落 0.3 个百分点，信⽤扩张偏弱、财政投放偏慢是主要拖
累。存款⽅⾯， 9 ⽉存款增加 1.77 万亿，同⽐少增 4400 亿，其中⾮银⾦融机构存款减少 1.91 万
亿、同⽐多减 8500 亿，也是连续第 5 个⽉同⽐少增，指向股市⾛弱背景下，居⺠存款搬家明显放
缓；财政存款⽅⾯，减少 4300 亿、同⽐少减 4100 亿，扣除政府债券后同⽐少减 622 亿，指向财
政投放仍偏慢。
⻛险提⽰：经济超预期下⾏，外部环境超预期，政策超预期。

本⽂来源：国盛宏观熊园团队

## Document 130: [005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005930] 삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정삼성전자(005930) 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-07
Pages: 9
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

www.imeritz.com

삼성전자 (005930)

Company Brief 
반도체/디스플레이 
Analyst 김선우 
sunwoo.kim@meritz.co.kr 
RA 우서현 
seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 2027년 메모리 판가·실적 상향 조정  
3Q26E 영업이익 106.1조원, 원화강세 불구 견조한 수준 기록 전망 
삼성전자의 3Q26 영업이익은 106.1조원으로 낮아진 시장 기대치 (104조원)를 소폭 
능가할 전망이다. 해당 분기동안 급격한 원화강세로 인해 실적 측면의 비우호적 환경
이 조성되었지만, 동사는 강건한 판매 정책에 기반해 메모리 판가가 산업 평균을 재
차 능가하며 견조한 실적 개선이 가능했다. 
동사 메모리 사업부의 3Q26 영업이익은 109조원으로 예상되며, 이중 DRAM과 
NAND는 각각 80조원 (OPM 78.5%) 과 29조원 (OPM 68.9%) 을 차지할 전망이다. 
DRAM과 NAND 출하량은 기존 예상치를 소폭 하회하는 +3.4%/+4.2% QoQ을 기
록하겠지만, 판가가 무려 +20.1%/19.0% QoQ 상승하며 실적 개선을 이끌겠다. 전
분기에 이어 ‘DS 특별경영성과급’ 충당금이 반영되며 손익에 일부 영향을 미치겠지
만, 동사 특유의 ‘수익성 극대화’ 판가 정책이 산업 우위 구도를 조성한다. 한편 LSI/
파운드리는 영업손실이 1.5조원을 기록하며 적자가 지속될 전망이다. 가동률 개선에 
기반한 사업 회복이 진행중이지만, 테일러팹 가동과 함께 비용 요인이 지속 출회할 
전망이다. 한편 반도체 외 기타 사업부의 험난한 실적 추이는 지속될 전망이다. SDC 
1.1조원 영업이익을 제외하고, DX (세트) 부문의 경우 MX -1.9조원, DA/VD -7,400
억원으로 영업적자 확대되리라 예상된다. 당사가 지난해 말부터 주장했듯, 세트 사업
은 메모리 등 부품원가 비중이 급격히 상승하는 가운데 향후 ‘판가 인상 → 판매량 
축소’의 ‘내구재화’ 변모 속 적자 최소화 노력만이 불가피할 전망이다.

1Q27 일부 모바일 DRAM 판가 +50% 폭등 예상. 적극적 매수 권고 
AGI를 향한 경쟁적이며 구조적 서버 수요 증가 불구, 스마트폰 중심의 B2C 응용처
는 유일한 판가저항처로 남아있다. 하지만 당사의 채널체크에 따르면, 메모리 업체들
의 지속적인 생산 전환 (LPDDR → 서버 DRAM 및 HBM)에 기반해 주요 고객 향 
모바일 메모리 판가는 무려 1Q27부터 50% 이상 QoQ 상향될 전망이다. 주요 고객
은 내년 20주년 플래그십 스마트폰을 제작하는 상황에서 판가 폭등을 감내해야하는 
상황이다. 최근 메모리 사이클 개선 속도에 대한 막연하며 진부한 우려가 존재하나, 
향후 1~2개월간 메모리 업체들에 대한 2027년 실적 추정치 상향과 함께 ‘성장·저평
가·주주환원 매력’이 동시에 발생할 전망이다. 동사의 2027년 영업이익 추정치를 기
존의 562조원에서 632조원으로 13% 상향조정하며, 시장 눈높이 역시 지속 상향되
리라 예상한다. 한편 배당 중심으로 발표됐던 동사의 주주환원의 경우, 모든 주주에게 
더 실효적인 ‘자사주 매입·소각’ 방안이 병행되는 방향으로 변모하리라 예상된다. 투
자의견 Buy와 적정주가 50만원을 유지한다. 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 7

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준) 
적정주가 (12개월) 500,000원 
현재주가 (10.6) 272,000원 
상승여력 83.8%

현재주가 
(26.10.02) 
1,354,000 
제약업종

◼ 3Q26 컨센서스 부합, 4Q26 성장폭 확대 전망 
◼ 5공장 Ramp-up 지속, 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목 
◼ 투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지

투자의견 매수, 목표주가 2,000,000원 유지 
목표주가는 DCF 방식으로 산출(WACC 8.1%, 영구성장률 3.5% 적용)

3Q26 Preview: 단기 부담에도 컨센서스 부합 전망 
3Q26 매출액 1조 3,380억원(+6.4% yoy, +1.3% qoq), 영업이익 5,890억원(-
7.0% yoy, +0.4% qoq, OPM 44.0%)으로 컨센서스에 부합하는 실적을 전
망함. 3분기 평균 원/달러 환율이 전분기 대비 약 5% 하락하고, 지난 5월 
파업에 따른 생산 차질 영향이 반영되나, 5공장 PPQ 물량의 매출 인식이 
시작되며 yoy, qoq 외형 성장은 이어질 것으로 예상함.  
파업 당시 생산이 지연된 물량은 3Q 중 추가 생산되어 4Q26부터 매출에 반
영될 전망임. 5공장은 다수 프로젝트의 PPQ가 진행 중이며, Ramp-up에 
따라 매출 기여도 점진적으로 확대될 것으로 예상함. 이에 4Q26에는 파
업 이연 물량 반영과 5공장 매출 확대에 따라 실적 성장폭이 확대될 전
망임. 2026년 연간 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 5조 5,310억원(+21.4% 
yoy), 2조 4,780억원(+19.8% yoy, OPM 44.8%)으로 전망함.

신규 수주 회복 기대, 중장기 성장 기반은 확대 중 
관세 관련 불확실성으로 둔화됐던 고객사와의 수주 논의가 재개되고 있음. 
10월 2일 유럽 소재 제약사와 935억원 규모의 CMO 계약을 추가 체결
했으며, 4Q26 신규 수주 회복 여부에 주목함.  
PolyPeptide 인수는 연내 거래 종결을 목표로 진행 중이며, 인수 완료 
시 펩타이드 분야로 CDMO 포트폴리오가 확대될 전망임. 6 공장도 연내 
착공을 목표로 준비 중임. 
한편, 노사 리스크는 단기 불확실성 요인임. 노조는 10/26부터 3주간 2
차 총파업을 예고함. 1차 대비 예정된 파업 기간이 길어진 만큼 실제 파
업 진행 시 생산 차질 규모가 주요 변수임. 다만 파업 개시 전까지 협상 
가능성이 열려 있어, 향후 노사 협상 진행 경과를 확인할 필요가 있음.

구분 Buy(매수) Marketperform(중
립) 
Underperform(매도) 
비율 93.2% 6.8% 0.0% 
산업 투자의견 
- Overweight(비중확대) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 초과 상승 예상 
- Neutral(중립) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률과 유사한 수준 예상 
- Underweight(비중축소) 
  : 향후 6개월간 업종지수상승률이 시장수익률 대비 하회 예상

기업 투자의견 
- Buy(매수)  
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 상승 예상 
- Marketperform(시장수익률) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 -10%p~10%p 주가 변동 예상 
- Underperform(시장수익률 하회) 
  : 향후 6개월간 시장수익률 대비 10%p 이상 주가 하락 예상

## Document 158: AIの雇用への影響をどう読むか

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIの雇用への影響をどう読むか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年 10 月 14 日 全13 頁

AI の雇用への影響をどう読むか 
一部の職種で狭まる若手の「入口」、経験を積む場をどう残すか

AI アナリティックリサーチ室 主任研究員 新田 尭之

[要約] 
 近年の実証研究では、米国を中心に、生成AI の影響を受けやすい一部の職種で若手の
雇用悪化が相次いで報告されている。悪化は主に採用の減少として表れており、最初
の職を得る「入口」が狭まっている。ただし、悪化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっ
ており、在宅勤務の広がりやコロナ禍の採用増の反動とも重なる。複数の要因が考え
られる中、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。 
 若手の「入口」が狭まる一方、米国全体では雇用者数が増えている。もっとも、 雇用
増の多くは民間教育・医療によるもので、幅広い産業には広がっていない。採用も離
職も少ないため、既に職に就いている人は職を失いにくいが、これから職を得る人や
転職を望む人にとっては空席が少ない。さらに、AI による省力化は、人員を減らすよ
り、業務の中身や外注を見直す形で進む例が多い。雇用者数だけでは、こうした仕事
の機会の変化は見えにくい。 
 今後の雇用を分けるのは、AI による値下げや品質向上で需要が広がったり、新しい業
務が生まれたりして、どれだけ人手が必要になるかである。ただし、新たな仕事が生
まれても、若手に回るとは限らない。41 カ国の多国籍企業の分析では、生成AI を導入
した企業の拠点で経験者の雇用が増え、若手の比率が下がった。経験者の多くは、入
口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手の採用を絞る判断は、個々の企業には合理的でも、続け
ば将来の経験者が育たない「育成の空洞化」を招きかねない。若手が経験を積む場を
どう残すかが問われており、社内で人を育てる日本企業も無縁ではいられないだろう。

1. はじめに 
近年、生成 AI の進化や普及を背景に、AI が雇用に与える影響について、米国を中心に実証
研究が蓄積されている（図表1）。 
図表 1 生成 AI が雇用に与える影響に関する近年の主な実証研究

### Page 3

1

3 / 13

例えば、スタンフォード大学の研究者らは、給与計算・人事サービスを手掛ける ADP の給与
記録を用いて米国の労働市場を分析し、主に次の 6 点を報告している 1。①AI による経済全体
にわたる広範な雇用喪失は確認されない。②一方、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、受けにく
い職種に比べて22～25 歳の雇用の伸びが弱く、年長層には同程度の差はみられない。③この若
年層の職種間格差は2026 年半ばにかけて拡大を続けた。④その差は主に離職の増加ではなく、
採用の減少による。⑤雇用の減少はAI が人の作業を代替する使い方の多い職種に集中し、仕事
を補完する使い方では雇用の横ばい・増加との関連がみられる。 ⑥調整は基本給よりも雇用の
面に表れている。 
こうした結果は、AI が雇用に与える影響を、全体の人数だけでなく、年齢や採用・離職、AI
の使われ方などに分けて捉える必要性を示している。そこで、本稿ではこうした実証研究や統
計データを基に、①雇用の悪化が目立つ層と、そのうちAI の影響と読める範囲（第 2 章） 、②
雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化（第3 章 ）、 ③今後の雇用を分ける要因（第4 章）
を検討する。

2. 悪化は「入口」で目立つが、AI だけでは説明できない 
（1）AI の影響を受けやすい職種の若年層を中心に悪化 
近年の実証研究を見ると、米国の若年層で雇用の悪化が目立つ。前述のスタンフォード大学
の研究では、22～25 歳の雇用者数は2026 年6 月にChatGPT 公開時の2022 年11 月を約2％下回
った一方、26 歳以上の各年齢層では増加し、特に35～40 歳では約11％多い（図表2）。 
図表 2 年齢層別に見た雇用者数の推移（米国、2022 年 11 月＝1）

（注）ADP の給与記録のうち、2021 年 1 月～2026 年 6 月に継続して観測される企業の 70 歳未満のフルタイム労
働者（毎月 350 万～500 万人） 。年齢層別人口の変化は未調整。 
（出所）Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]より大和総研作成（画像の加工・仮訳は大和総研）

1 Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]. AI 曝露はEloundou et al. [2024] の指標のうち GPT-4 による
判定の値で、職業を五つの群に分けて比べている。

### Page 4

1

4 / 13

ただし、若年層の雇用が職種を問わず悪化したわけではない。同研究の職種別の推計では、
AI への曝露（仕事の内容がAI で代替・支援されうる度合い）が最も高い職種群の22～25 歳の
雇用の伸びは、2022 年11 月から2026 年6 月までに、最も低い職種群の雇用の伸びを約18％ポ
イント下回った。若年層の中でも、AI の影響を受けやすい職種で悪化が目立つ。 
さらに、米国国勢調査局の研究者らは、大学の卒業記録と雇用の行政記録を 接続し、専攻別
に卒業生の就職状況を比べた。これによると、 AI への曝露が最も高い専攻の卒業生は、
ChatGPT 公開直後から、卒業翌四半期に就業している割合が曝露の低い専攻の卒業生と比べて
低下し始め、2024 年以降は約5％ポイント低い2。 
米国の約6,500 万人分の職歴データを企業単位で分析した研究でも、生成AI の導入企業では
若手（職位の低い層）の雇用が2023 年初めから6 四半期で比較対象の企業より約9％減った。
これも主に採用の減少によるという 3。求人の側では、求人サイト Indeed の分析で、テック職
で 5 年以上の経験を求める割合の上昇が確認されている4。 
ニューヨーク連銀が集計したデータからも、若年大卒者の悪化は確認できる。22～27 歳の若
年大卒者の失業率は、2018 年までほぼ一貫して全労働者を下回っていたが、2019 年以降はおお
むね上回るようになり、2026 年 6 月は 5.7％と全労働者（4.1％）を 1.6％ポイント上回る。大
卒でない若年層（22～27 歳）の失業率が 2025 年秋を頂点に下がり始めた後も、若年大卒者の
失業率は高止まりしている（図表3）5。 
図表 3 全労働者と若年層の失業率（米国）

卒者は 22～27 歳の学士以上で、いずれも在学中の人を除く。

若年大卒者の失業率を専攻別に見ると、2024 年の失業率はコンピューター工学が 7.8％、コ
ンピューター科学が 7.0％と、全専攻（4.2％）を大きく上回り、73 専攻の上位 5 位以内に入
る。一方、就職後の年収中央値は 9 万ドル、8.7 万ドルと全専攻（5.8 万ドル）を大きく上回
り、大卒資格を要しない職に就く不完全就業率も低い。就業者の年収水準の高さと、失業率の
高さが併存している（図表4）。

なお、オンラインのフリーランス市場でも、AI の影響を受ける分野では、補完的とみなされ
る技能の分野を含め、経験の浅い人向けの案件が、影響を受けない分野に比べて相対的に減っ
たと報告されている 6。年齢と経験の浅さは同じ区分ではないが、若手の 雇用悪化と経験の浅
い人向けの案件の減少は、最初の仕事を得る機会、いわば「入口」が狭まっている点で共通す
る。 
（2）ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化や在宅勤務の広がりなども重なる 
ただし、こうした悪化の原因すべてを生成AI の影響に帰するのは早計であろう。第一に、悪
化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっていた。例えば、米国のソフトウェア開発職の求人は、
2022 年 2 月をピークに急減し、ChatGPT 公開時には既にピークを3 分の 1 あまり下回っていた
（図表5）。 
ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された求人データの分析でも、AI 曝露の高い職種の求人の
相対的な減少は2022 年より前に始まっており、2022 年以降に傾向の明確な変化は見られない。

6 Teutloff et al. [2025]. 受注・収入の減少はHui, Reshef, and Zhou [2024] も参照。

### Page 6

1

6 / 13

同じ分析では、高曝露職の中で若手（職位の低い層）向けと経験者向けの求人がほぼ並行して
動いており、求人の減少は入口の仕事に集中していないという 7。 
図表 5 ソフトウェア開発職の求人指数（米国）

（出所）Indeed Hiring Lab より大和総研作成

第二に、在宅勤務の広がりである。ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された分析によると、
米国の在宅勤務が可能な職業では、若手と年長の大卒者の失業率の差が2019 年から 2024 年に
約 1.4％ポイント広がった 8。これは、在宅勤務が難しい職業の同期間の拡大幅（約0.5％ポイ
ント）を大きく上回る。同分析は、在宅勤務では若手への OJT や上司からの助言が難しくなる
ため、企業が経験の浅い人材の採用をためらいやすくなると指摘している。 米英加豪の採用記
録約 2.4 億件の分析でも、在宅勤務のしやすさを同時に考慮すると、AI 曝露と採用者に占める
若手（職位の低い層）の比率との関係は大きく弱まり、多くの場合、統計的に有意 な関係は確
認できなくなった 9。 
米国外の研究も、若手の雇用の減少を AI だけでは説明していない。デンマークでは、AI 曝
露の高いいくつかの職で若手の雇用が減ったものの、研究者らはその減少はAI チャットボット
を導入した企業によるものではないとしている 10。さらに、韓国銀行の研究者の分析によれば、
韓国では 2022 年 6 月から 2026 年 6 月に 15～29 歳の雇用が 28.5 万人減り、その 94％は AI の
影響を受けやすい産業で生じた 11。同研究は、コロナ禍の採用増の反動や経験者中心の採用、
在宅勤務の広がりなども作用した可能性があると指摘している。

7 Audoly, Guerin, and Topa [2026]. 求人情報などを収集・分析するLightcast の米国の求人データを用い、
AI 曝露はAnthropic が作成した、AI の実際の利用状況を反映した作業単位の指標による。若手（職位の低い
層）向け・経験者向けの区分は Lightcast が求人の記載から判定したもの。求人の数の分析であり、実際に誰
が採用されたかは捉えない点で、第 2 章（1）の研究とは対象が異なる。 
8 Emanuel, Harrington, and Pallais [2026]. 大卒者の18～28 歳と29 歳以上の失業率の差の2019 年からの
変化で、数値は同記事のチャートデータによる。 
9 Lambert and Schindler [2026]. 米英加豪の2017～2025 年のデータで、分析対象は、職名と職歴から判定し
た職位の下位 2 区分が採用者に占める比率。 
10 Humlum and Vestergaard [2026]. AI の影響を受けやすい 11 職業の約 2.5 万人への調査を2024 年 12 月まで
の行政記録と接続。新しい業務や時間の使い方は自己申告による。 
11 Oh and Oh [2026]. 雇用は国民年金の加入者数。AI の影響を受けやすい産業は、AI 曝露（Eloundou et al. 
[2024]）で全産業を 4 分位に分けた上位2 分位の産業。

### Page 7

1

7 / 13

そして先に紹介したスタンフォード大学の研究では 、在宅勤務や金利への感応度を調整して
も、22～25 歳の高曝露職と低曝露職の雇用の伸びの差は大きく変わらないが、学歴構成も含め
て調整するとその差は調整前の約 18％ポイントから約 8％ポイントに縮み、統計的に有意な関
係は確認できなくなる12。 
以上のように、一部の職種で若手の雇用が悪化していることは確認できるが、その原因を生
成 AI に求めるには慎重さが必要である。その影響を見極めるには、ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化
と、公開後に加わった可能性のある変化を分けて考える必要がある。また 、足元の弱さをコロ
ナ禍の採用増の反動だけで説明できるとは限らない。複数の要因が考えられる中で、本稿で取
り上げた研究でも、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。

3. 雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化 
仕事を得る機会を考えるには、雇用者数が増えているかどうかに加え、どの分野で増えてい
るのか、新たな採用がどの程度行われているのかを見る必要がある。また、AI による省力化も、
人員数を変えずに業務や外注の見直しとして進む場合がある。以下では、これら三つの側面を
確認する。 
（1）雇用増は民間教育・医療に偏る 
米国では、ChatGPT が公開された2022 年 11 月から2026 年 8 月までに、情報や専門・事業サ
ービスなど、AI の影響を受けやすい職種の多い産業で雇用は減少した。一方、全体の雇用増を
支えたのは民間教育・医療である。同期間の雇用増約 480 万人のうち、約 3 分の 2（約 325 万
人）を占めた。ただし、直近 1 年の全体の雇用増は約 60 万人にとどまり、そのうち約 54 万人
を民間教育・医療が占める（図表6）。 
図表 6 業種別に見た雇用者数の増減（米国）

（注）その他は全体から掲載した業種を差し引いた残差。 
（出所）米労働統計局（BLS）より大和総研作成

12 この研究では、学歴構成は AI が仕事に影響する経路も反映している可能性があるため、その違いを調整し
て差が縮んでも、AI 以外の要因で説明できたとは限らない、としている。

### Page 8

1

8 / 13

民間教育・医療の雇用者数の伸びは、コロナ禍で落ち込んだ反動にもみえる。ただ、コロナ
禍前の2020 年2 月から見ても、雇用増約680 万人の半分（約340 万人）を占めており、雇用増
の最大の担い手であることは変わらない。全体の雇用増が、幅広い産業での雇用拡大を意味す
るわけではない。 
（2）低採用・低離職の下で就職・転職の機会は限られる 
雇用者数の動きと、新たに職を得る機会の違いは、採用と離職からも確認できる。 米国の求
人労働異動調査（JOLTS）によると、2026 年 1～7 月の採用率は平均 3.3％と、コロナ禍前の
2017～19 年（3.8％）を下回り、失業率が 7～8％台だった 2012～13 年並みに低い（図表 7）。
同じ期間の比較では、離職率も3.7％から3.3％に下がり、自己都合退職率（2.2％→2.0％）も
解雇率（1.2％→1.1％）も低下した。採用率の低さは若手だけの現象ではなく、労働市場全体
にみられる。 
図表 7 採用率と離職率（米国）

（出所）BLS より大和総研作成

離職が少なければ、欠員を埋める採用も生まれにくい。就職・転職の機会が限られる中、 先
行きへの不安から今の職にとどまる動きは「ジョブハギング（仕事にしがみつくこと） 」と呼
ばれ、2025 年 8 月ごろから米国の報道で広く取り上げられている13。米国の履歴書作成サイト
が2026 年2 月に行った調査では、自らをジョブハギングの状態にあると回答した就業者が57％
と、半年前の 45％から増えた。その 7 割は、今後半年以内に AI が自分の雇用に影響すること
を懸念していた 14。就職の入口に立つ人にとっては、いわば「空席の少ない市場」である。 
（3）AI による省力化の影響は、人員の削減より仕事の中身の見直しに表れている 
AI による省力化についても、雇用者数だけでは変化を捉えにくい。OECD が日本を含む7 カ国

13 例えば、Fortune “Workers are ‘job hugging’ in a stagnant labor market, but growing resentment 
means they could bail as soon as the next Great Resignation comes”（2025 年8 月 18 日）。 
14 ResumeBuilder.com “6 in 10 Workers Are Clinging To Their Jobs as Job Hugging Soars in 2026”
（2026 年2 月11 日）。米国の就業者2,188 人へのオンライン調査で、ジョブハギングを、会社への愛着では
なく不安から今の職にとどまることとしている。

### Page 9

1

9 / 13

の中小企業に行った調査では、生成 AI を使う企業の 83％が人員の必要数に影響はないと答え
た。必要な人員数の減少（9％）より、従業員の業務負担（33％）や外注への依存（14％）の
減少を挙げる企業が多い 15。社内の人員数が変わらなくても、外部に依頼する仕事は減りうる。 
前出のデンマークの研究では、時間を節約できた利用者の 85％が、その時間を他の業務に充
てると答え、雇用主が特段の施策を講じていない職場でも、利用者の8％が AI の出力の品質確
認など全く新しい業務を担っていた。研究者らは、こうした状況が、業務の見直しなど計測さ
れにくい投資が先行し、生産性の伸びが遅れて表れる「J カーブ」の初期段階と整合的だとし
ている 16。AI による省力化が、直ちに人員や労働時間の削減につながるのではなく、仕事の配
分を変える形で進んでいることがうかがえる。

4. 新たな仕事が生まれても、若手に回るとは限らない 
第 2 章で紹介した研究では、若手の雇用悪化は、主に「解雇」ではなく「採用されない」形
で表れている。この状態が続くかは、AI による省力化で生まれる余力を企業がどの業務に振り
向け、新たな需要がどれだけ人手を必要とするかにかかっている。 
省力化の後の展開は、主に三つの経路として整理できる 17。①人数を変えずに仕事の中身が
変わる。②既存の社員で対応できる範囲が広がり、採用・外注の抑制や人員削減につながる。
③値下げや品質向上による需要の拡大、新しい業務の創出が人手を必要とし、配置転換や採用
につながる。 
AI 導入後の雇用の動きには、企業規模による違いも見られる。デンマークの研究では、AI を
導入した小規模企業で新たな採用が抑えられ、雇用の増えるペースが鈍ったと報告されている。
一方、大規模企業では、同様の雇用の伸びの鈍化は確認されなかった18。 
②と③は、同じ企業の中で同時に進みうる。41 カ国の多国籍企業を対象とした研究は、AI導
入企業で増えた雇用がどの層に向かったかを示している。生成AI を導入した企業の拠点では、
ChatGPT 公開直後から若手（職位の低い層）の比率が下がり始め、2026 年 3 月には導入してい
ない企業の拠点での比率を 1.9％ポイント下回った（図表 8）19。この比率低下の主因は、若手

の雇用減少ではなく、ChatGPT 公開から約 1 年半後に始まった経験者の雇用増加である。この
研究の著者らは、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、AI が若手の仕事を省く一方で経験者への需
要を広げたと解釈している。需要が広がっても、その担い手が若手になるとは限らないことを
示す結果である。

③の経路を示す研究もある。米国の上場企業では、AI 投資を増やした企業ほど、主に製品の
革新を通じて売上と雇用が伸びたとされる 20。デンマークの研究で報告された、AI の出力の品
質確認などの新しい業務も③の芽といえる。 
採用の構成も、AI の使い方によって変わりうる。AI を使う既存社員が若手の入口の仕事を担
えば、採用は経験者に寄りうる。逆に、AI の支援で経験の浅い人に任せられる仕事が増えれば、

20 Babina et al. [2024]. 2010～2018 年の非テック上場企業の分析。効果は規模の大きい企業に集中する。他
社からの需要の移転も含みうるが、AI 投資の多い産業では産業全体でも売上と雇用が増えたとされる。

### Page 11

1

11 / 13

若手を採る利点は高まりうる。AI が経験の差を縮めるこうした作用は、「専門性の平準化」と
呼ばれる 21。 
もっとも、これまでの実証研究では、採用が経験者に寄る動きが目立つ。この 動きが続けば、
懸念されるのは「育成の空洞化」である。 経験者の多くは、入口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手採
用の絞り込みは、個々の企業には合理的でも、経済全体では将来の経験者層 を薄くしかねない。
AI の支援と経験者の指導を組み合わせ、若手が経験を積む場を残す工夫が求められる。 
日本企業もこの問題と無縁ではいられないだろう。日本では少子高齢化で人手不足が続き、
新卒を一括採用して社内で育てる企業が多い。このため、影響は採用数より、採る職種の構成
や、人手の足りない部署への配置転換に先に表れる可能性がある 22。とはいえ、採用数が保た
れても、AI が入口の仕事を担えば、若手が経験を積む場は細りうる。問われているのは「AI で
若手の仕事をどこまで省けるか」ではなく、 「AIを使いながら若手をどう育てるか」である。

ECONOMIC INSIGHT
• The recent tendency of some firms to bundle into prices
card-acceptance costs that had previously been surcharged
– and then some, in some cases – demonstrates a ‘cost-
plus’ approach to pricing. To the extent they can pass on
these costs through to prices, it suggests that demand was

strong enough to bear it. The RBA will take this as a signal
that demand is still outstripping supply.
• This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. Base wages
growth has been moderate for some years, though the
above-average increase in award wages this year will add
a little. Not all firms will seek to pass these cost increases
on, but some will, including because they have limited
scope to absorb the cost increase into margins.
• A cost-plus mentality has drawbacks, including that it

limits the imagination away from finding alternative,
efficiency-improving responses. It also accords with
recent academic research suggesting that firms form
expectations and set prices according to what is
happening in their own industry and experience, while
putting little weight on economy-wide inflation or central
bank credibility. This is an unpleasant message for many
consumers or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when
supply shocks abound.

The recent banning of card surcharges has caused
considerable upset this month, even though it is a reversion
to historical and international practice. The card schemes
used to impose “no-surcharge” rules, until the RBA banned
these in 2003, and many other countries still have them. The
turnaround represents the RBA throwing in the towel on its
longstanding effort to use price incentives to get people to
choose low-cost payment methods. Surcharges no longer

made sense in a largely cashless economy. In any case, least-
cost routing puts the power to choose lower-cost methods in
the hands of the merchants who actually bear the cost.
In most cases, firms will simply roll the surcharge into the sticker
price, leaving the total cost to the consumer unchanged, just
more transparent. We also understand that some large retailers
have absorbed the cost – if they were surcharging in the first
place, which many did not. (The RBA estimated that around

16% of businesses imposed surcharges.) Inevitably, though,
some firms have lifted their prices by more than any reasonable
estimate of the merchant service fee that the surcharge was
meant to cover. And because this is Australia, of course takeaway
coffee has been the centre of consumer discontent.
Higher prices for one’s caffeine fix was unlikely to be the main
instigator of this month’s slump in consumer sentiment. Petrol
‘Cost-Plus’ costs
Forecast changes

• A full update to our forecasts was published in our October
Market Outlook  on Thursday 15 October.
Luci Ellis
Chief Economist, Westpac Group
prices are typically more salient for cost-of-living concerns, and
the before-and-after picture on the RBA announcement that week
shows that higher interest rates also contributed to the decline.
In some senses, though, the post-surcharge prices and the
RBA’s decisions are linked: businesses passing on increased

costs into their own prices – and expecting to be able to
make that increase stick – is exactly the kind of inflationary
environment the RBA is leaning against. Firms have been
subjected to an array of non-labour cost escalations, from
pandemic-era supply chain issues to electricity to fuel to
water to building supplies. And IT equipment prices, which
for decades were a reliable source of cost reductions, are
nowadays flat to up. Passing these costs on, leaving margins

intact, demonstrates that demand is not weak enough to
prevent this, and signals that firms’ inflationary expectations
might be higher than the RBA desires. The RBA will take this
as a sign that demand is still outstripping supply.
This is mostly a story about non-labour costs. While the Fair
Work Commission did hand down an above-average increase in
award wages this year and this was probably passed through
to prices in hospitality and other labour-intensive sectors, base

wages growth has been moderate for some years. Business
surveys point to non-labour cost escalation being the real issue
lately. This is why we were puzzled by the passage in the RBA
minutes this week suggesting that cost pressures reported
in business surveys might imply that the unemployment rate
consistent with inflation at target (the NAIRU) has risen. It is
a convenient belief, though, if unemployment is starting to
overshoot your forecasts, as the latest labour market data have

done compared with the RBA’s August forecasts.
Not all firms can pass on cost increases. Most small firms do
not have the market power, though coffee may be an exception
because convenience induces a kind of local market power. And
as noted above, large firms with everyday-low-price strategies
will instead try to find savings elsewhere in the supply chain.
Still, some firms will seek to pass these cost increases on.
Some observers might see this as corporate greed, but another

way to frame this strategy is simply as a ‘cost-plus’ mentality.
“Entrenched expectations that cost increases
can and should be passed on are precisely
what tight monetary policy is leaning against,
but only indirectly."

3
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
It turns out that cost-plus pricing is especially common in
areas where regulators determine the price – water and other
utilities being examples where costs and prices have escalated
a lot in recent years.
There are limits to firms’ ability to absorb costs, too. The

failures of so many building firms a few years ago, squeezed
between post-pandemic cost escalation, delivery delays and
fixed-price contracts, were a case in point. Normally, there is
a contractual response, and it need not end in insolvency. The
episode does illustrate, however, that it is unrealistic to expect
firms never to pass cost increases through to prices.
The cost-plus mentality does have its drawbacks. It limits
the imagination away from finding responses that might save

costs elsewhere to absorb the cost hike, and in so doing, boost
efficiency. It was equally limiting in times of soft demand: in
the years before the pandemic, many firms professed a belief
that, because they couldn’t get price increases to stick, they
could not possibly accept cost increases, including in wages.
Thus, Australia ended up with wages growth too low to be
consistent with inflation at target, and the peculiar spectacle
of a central bank governor complaining that wages growth

was too low. And if labour remains cheap, why invest to
economise on it and thereby boost productivity?
A cost-plus mentality also amplifies the pass-through of
supply shocks. This is not quite the same thing as the ‘inflation
expectations’ seen in standard economic models of inflation,
but it is related. The stronger the tendency towards cost-plus
pricing, the worse the inflation response to a supply shock.
This is also true when inflation expectations are high, but the

distinction matters. The recent academic literature includes
some fascinating empirical evidence  from New Zealand that
suggests that beliefs about current and future overall inflation
have little bearing on many firms’ pricing decisions. As related
work using French data shows, firms’ pricing choices are
shaped less by economy-wide inflation developments than by
what is happening to their costs and in their industry.
That adds up to economy-wide behaviour that looks a lot

like firms (in aggregate) setting prices according to expected
inflation (in aggregate), but under the hood something
quite different is going on. If firms’ beliefs about future
economy-wide inflation do not matter much in their pricing
decisions, then neither does the credibility of a central bank’s
commitment to return inflation to target. The mechanism to
get inflation down then must be simply to weaken demand
enough to convince firms that cost-plus will not fly in the

market. This is an unpleasant message for many consumers
or anyone in an interest-sensitive sector when supply shocks
abound. It does, however, imply that if supply shocks fade
or even self-correct, so will high inflation. If and when that
happens, tight monetary policy can be unwound.
The deeper question is whether cost-plus price dynamics can
be reduced. That is more likely to be a question for regulators
than for central banks.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
THE WEEK THAT WAS
Cliff Notes: getting the balance right
bias towards an October or December decision. Williams
stated he believes one more hike would prove sufficient in
this cycle given known information. And Waller also showed
confidence, highlighting that elevated long-term yields are

doing some of the FOMC’s work, restricting demand in interest
rate sensitive sectors. We hold a similar view, anticipating that
one more hike by year end and a little time will bring inflation
near the 2.0%yr medium-term target. However, as discussed
in this month’s Market Outlook , capacity pressures are likely
to see inflation consistently above target, rather than below,
in coming years – particularly if, as we suspect, capacity in the
housing sector remains tight. This will mean the FOMC have to

remain modestly restrictive over the forecast horizon and will
also bias up long-term yields.
In Asia, there was one release that stood out, China’s trade
account. At USD133.5bn, the September result was a whisker
inside January 2025’s record high of USD137.9bn. More
impressively, throughout 2025 and 2026 year-to-date the
surplus has now averaged USD100.9bn, almost 3 times the
average of 2019, prior to the pandemic. The geographic breadth
of export growth is as impressive as the headline annual gain of

36.3%yr, even more so given the ongoing strength in Chinese
investment in neighbouring nations’ production chains. In time,
both trade and income receipts from offshore investment should
buoy the renminbi, although for now continued outbound
foreign investment should moderate the pace of gains against
other nations in the region, and likely versus the US dollar.
Another key trend assessed in October’s Market Outlook is the
evolution of producer prices across Asia, and the likelihood

of the disinflationary benefit the west has received in recent
decades continuing. Historically, labour cost arbitrage was
one of the key drivers of declining goods prices. While
still possible, we expect the next phase of manufacturing
development to instead be driven by capital, technology and
energy. Many end users and the manufacturers undertaking
production are also increasingly prioritising resilience, security
and specialisation over cost minimisation. It is therefore more

probably future supply-chain developments will help contain
rising costs through productivity than drive sustained price
declines for the goods bought by consumers and businesses.
Australia’s key data release this week was the September
Labour Force Survey . Employment surprised to the
downside, –39.3k. However, a 0.2ppt decline in labour force
participation from August’s near-historic high largely offset.
The unemployment rate therefore held at 4.7% after August

was revised up.
Residual seasonality may be in the mix. Regardless,
the take home point from the update is that the labour
market is continuing to ease as employment growth runs
below working-age population growth. We expect the
unemployment rate to grind higher into 2027 as tighter
financial conditions and cost-of-living pressures restrict
activity growth to a below-trend pace. Although, our forecast
peak is near at 4.8%, due by Q2 2027.
This update on the labour market complicates the

communication challenge for the RBA. The September minutes
were firmly on the hawkish side, with the discussion focussing
on materialising upside risks to inflation. But, with the
unemployment rate now through the RBA’s NAIRU assumption
of 4.6%, Governor Bullock’s “a little tight” descriptor for the
labour market is becoming harder to defend. Still, with a clear
focus on inflation risks – which also led us to revise up our
near-term CPI forecasts in the October Market Outlook  – we

still expect a 25bp rate hike at the November meeting.
Over in the US, the headline CPI rose 0.6% as expected in
September, taking the annual rate up to 3.7%yr. Energy prices
were a key contributor (5.2%, 20.4%yr), while core inflation
was a touch above consensus in the month at 0.3% (2.6%yr).
Within the core components, core goods prices gained 0.3%
after being broadly unchanged over the past 6 months
(0.7%yr). Core services meanwhile rose 0.4% (3.1%yr) on a

0.4% increase in shelter (3.2%yr), a 0.6% gain for transport
services and a 0.3% increase in medical care services (2.7%yr
and 2.5%yr respectively). Further, the subsequent release
of the PPI pointed to continued upstream price pressures,
with the headline and core measures up 6.3%yr and 5.0%yr
respectively. The latest Beige Book provided colour on these
developments, firms across multiple districts citing tariffs,
insurance and commodity prices as ongoing sources of cost

pressure. Guiding on the risks, many respondents expect
further price increases as contracts/hedges roll off.
Given their telegraphed concerns over inflation, there is
enough signal in these reports to warrant a follow-up hike
at the October FOMC meeting. Though it seems Committee
members believe they are near the end of the tightening cycle
and so have time on their side. This week, Daly and Bowman
both showed patience in assessing the trend, with no clear

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
FOCUS ON NEW ZEALAND
Downward revisions to 2027 outlook
This week we have updated our New Zealand economic
outlook for the next year, in particular reflecting the latest
developments in the energy market and key financial
variables (long-term interest rates and the exchange rate).

After last week’s marking down of our forecast for through-
year GDP growth in 2026 to 2.5%yr, this week we have
reduced our forecast of growth in 2027 by 0.3ppts to 2.7%yr.
This downward revision reflects a less positive assessment
of the outlooks for consumption and investment (including
residential investment) considering the more elevated path
assumed for energy prices and a higher near-term track for
long-term interest rates (as discussed in our updated Market

Outlook, published earlier this week).
A partial offset to weaker growth in domestic demand is
provided by a slightly more positive outlook for exports
(particularly exports of manufactured goods and tourism
inflows) because of a downward revision to our expectations
for the NZ dollar. We now expect NZD/USD to end this
year around current levels at 0.56 – 3c lower than expected
previously – before appreciating to around 0.64 over the
course of 2027 as interest rate differentials with the US

gradually close and as the USD appreciates. We think that the
low value of the NZ dollar – in trade-weighted terms now at a
17-year low – helps to explain the recent strong performance
in the previously moribund manufacturing sector, as well as
tourist inflows from key markets such as Australia.
Last week we raised our forecast for CPI inflation at year end
to 4.3%yr, largely on account of recent developments in global
energy prices (although the weak NZ dollar has compounded the

effect). This week we have slightly revised up our forecast for
inflation for the first half of 2027. But because energy prices are
assumed to decline in 2027 from what is now a higher starting
level, our 2027 year-end forecast for inflation has nudged
down 0.1ppts to 1.6%yr. Needless to say, forecasts of headline
inflation, in particular, are subject to significant revision given the
uncertain outlook for the Middle East conflict and uncertainty

about how that will be reflected in global energy prices.
Ahead of next week’s very important Q3 CPI report – which
could prove decisive – we have made no changes to our
forecast for the RBNZ’s policy settings. So, for now we
continue to think that despite the higher near-term inflation
outlook the RBNZ will stick with the strategy communicated
in the September Monetary Policy Statement, i.e. that at the
vv28 October meeting it will pause to assess the impact of this

year’s 50bps of policy tightening, before considering a further
upward adjustment in December.
Turning to this week’s data, the news from the housing market
remained decidedly downbeat. House sales fell a further 4% in
September – the seventh consecutive month of decline – to be
12% lower than a year earlier. With the stock of listings at an
11-year high, the median selling time remains elevated. And
so unsurprisingly, house prices fell for a fourth consecutive

month and are now down 1.6%yr. Prices remain especially
soft in the North Island, with prices down more than 3%yr in
Auckland and more than 4%yr in Wellington. Given upward
pressures on mortgage rates, broader strength in the economy
– including a stronger labour market – will likely be required to
reverse this trend. On that score, MBIE’s measure of online job
advertisements was little changed in September, continuing
the flat trend since the beginning of this year.

In other news, retail spending, as measured by electronic card
payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September following an
unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This was a weaker result than
we had expected based on the recent sawtooth pattern and
our own internal data. We should treat these data with some
suspicion given that for quite a while now the Stats NZ series
seems not to have reflected the spending seen through banks’
own card spending networks, perhaps suggesting changes in the

payments arena may be leaving the official sector data behind.
Even so, unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding late in
the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However,
core spending, which excludes spending on fuel and vehicles,
fell 0.7%m/m. While spending on consumables (groceries)
increased modestly in September, this was at the expense of
spending on ‘nice to haves’. Spending on hospitality suffered a
Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist

further 2.3%mth fall following a 1.9%mth decline in August,
and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline
reported in August. Spending on durable goods also fell
slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September

months, retail spending over the September quarter still
eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased
0.4%. Given higher prices, at face value this points to a weak
quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more
light on pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth
noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in the
official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could
point to somewhat stronger growth. Purchases of big-ticket

items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture
and appliances – are underrepresented in the electronic card
spending survey.
Given subdued domestic demand, local hospitality businesses
have relied on foreign visitors to drive growth in spending.
And the good news is that visitor arrivals increased to a new
post-Covid high in August to be up 8%yr. Arrivals of Chinese
citizens have grown by almost a third to sit near pre-Covid
levels in recent months, while arrivals of US citizens grew

17%yr. However, the Middle East conflict has impacted
long-haul arrivals from Europe (UK arrivals are down 8%yr).
Meanwhile, inflows of migrants remain at modest levels,
totalling around 20,000 in the year to August – about half
what might be expected on average over the cycle.
News from the manufacturing sector remained positive in
September, with the BusinessNZ Manufacturing PMI rising
to a 3-month high of 56.5 in September. Improvement was
seen across all subcomponents of the index, with the new

orders index rising 1.8pts to 57.0. We think that the recovery in
manufacturing this year owes much to the weakness of the NZ
dollar – especially against the Australian dollar – and activity
associated with a strong domestic primary sector and an
emerging recovery in domestic residential construction activity.
Last but certainly not least, ahead of next week’s all-important
Q3 CPI, the Selected Prices report for September was firmer
than expected. That was mainly due to larger than expected

increases in airfares and holiday accommodation costs, which
offset softer than expected food prices.
Accounting for the September prices data, we’ve left our
forecast for Q3 inflation  at 0.7%. That would see the annual
inflation rate dropping back to 3.7%, down from 4.1% in the
year to June. Underlying that move has been a moderation in
fuel prices after they rocketed higher in the wake of the Middle
East war. That’s balanced against increases in council rates,

building costs and food prices.
Our forecast is lower than the RBNZ’s September MPS
forecast for a 0.8% rise (+3.9%yr). However, with some large
swings in volatile items like fuel, the RBNZ will likely focus
on measures of core inflation. Core inflation was already at
firm levels in the June quarter, with key measures sitting in
the upper part of the RBNZ’s target band. We expect that
they will take another step higher in September, to sit around

3%. Upside surprises in core inflation would be an important
development for the RBNZ ahead of the October OCR review,
as this could suggest that the lower NZD and higher oil prices
are boosting underlying price pressures by more and sooner
than they may have expected.

### Page 7

Q3 is likely to produce a very similar outcome to Q2, with 
quarterly growth of around 1.0% holding the annual rate at 
4.3%yr. Year-to-date, growth will remain around 4.7%ytd 
thanks to the Q1 result.
The detail and partial data for September will again highlight 
the weak state of the consumer and domestic-oriented 
businesses, but also the incredible strength of exports. With 
the trade surplus back at record highs, there are limits to the 
contribution possible from net exports hence, necessitating 
a strengthening of domestic demand if aggregate growth is 
to continue at the rate authorities intend into 2027. Expect 
additional stimulus through the turn of the year.

Key points
• The Cotality home value index –
covering all of Australia – fell 1.1% in
September following a downwardly
revised declines of 1.2% in August

and 1.4% in July. Annual growth
stalled flat with prices nationally now
5.2% below their peak in March.
• Declines continue to be widespread.
Across the major cities, prices fell
1.4% in Sydney, 1.5% in Brisbane,
1.3% in Adelaide, and 1.2% in Perth
with Melbourne recording a slightly
milder 0.7% decline. Sydney and
Melbourne continue to see more
pronounced declines with prices now
down 7%yr and 6.2%yr respectively.
• Preliminary estimates show the number

of sales, in rolling 3mth terms, down
3.9%qtr and 17.4%yr. The pace of
turnover falls has moderated from
the rapid 8.6%qtr recorded in July.

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year
ago, albeit still not high compared to long run averages
(reflecting the extremely tight starting point for the Brisbane
market).
• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

also marked down from 0.8% to 1.1%, annual price growth
dropping to 6.5%yr. The price correction began later in
Adelaide but is now running at a similar pace to prices
nationally.
• Perth recorded a 1.2% decline but also saw big downward
revisions to previous months (August from 0.8% to –1.7%
and July from –1.3% to –1.8%). At 10.1%yr, annual growth
is still solidly positive but was tracking at over 25%yr earlier
in the year. As with Adelaide and Brisbane, the detail shows

a more even spread of declines across price tiers. However,
turnover is showing a milder pull-back in Adelaide.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

Vietnam’s economic success story has continued

◼

 It is

 level of

, but

have 
 deteriorat

in recent months. Public and external debt ratios 
 remain  
moderate.

 ,

the consequences have been 
 less severe  
than expected  
so far
 . Economic 
activity

 strong
 ly
, notably because the export
